$-\dfrac{3}{c} = 4 \Rightarrow c = -\dfrac{3}{4}$

Substituting $c = -\dfrac{3}{4}$ into the equation of K, we get:

$\dfrac{x}{-3/4} + \dfrac{y}{3} = 1 \Rightarrow -\dfrac{4x}{3} + \dfrac{y}{3} = 1 \Rightarrow 4x - y + 3 = 0$

The distance $d$ between the parallel lines $4x - y - 20 = 0$ and $4x - y + 3 = 0$ is given by:

$d = \dfrac{|C_1 - C_2|}{\sqrt{A^2 + B^2}}$

$d = \dfrac{|-20 - 3|}{\sqrt{4^2 + (-1)^2}} = \dfrac{23}{\sqrt{17}}$

Answer: $\dfrac{23}{\sqrt{17}}$

</details>

---

### Q97. MHT CET 2024 (02 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The equation of the line, through $A(1,2,3)$ and perpendicular to the vector $2 \hat{i}+\hat{j}-\hat{k}$ and $\hat{i}+3 \hat{j}+2 \hat{k}$, is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\bar{r}=(\hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k})+\lambda(\hat{i}+\hat{j}+\hat{k})$
- **(B)** $\bar{r}=(\hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k})+\lambda(\hat{i}-\hat{j}-\hat{k})$
- **(C)** $\quad \bar{r}=(\hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k})+\lambda(\hat{i}+\hat{j}-\hat{k})$
- **(D)** $\bar{r}=(\hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k})+\lambda(\hat{i}-\hat{j}+\hat{k})$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\bar{r}=(\hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k})+\lambda(\hat{i}-\hat{j}+\hat{k})$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The position vector of the point $A(1, 2, 3)$ is $\vec{a} = \hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}$.

The line is perpendicular to the vectors $\vec{v}_1 = 2\hat{i} + \hat{j} - \hat{k}$ and $\vec{v}_2 = \hat{i} + 3\hat{j} + 2\hat{k}$. Therefore, its direction vector $\vec{b}$ is parallel to the cross product of $\vec{v}_1$ and $\vec{v}_2$.

$\vec{b} = \vec{v}_1 \times \vec{v}_2 = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 2 & 1 & -1 \\ 1 & 3 & 2 \end{vmatrix}$

$\vec{b} = \hat{i}(2 + 3) - \hat{j}(4 + 1) + \hat{k}(6 - 1) = 5\hat{i} - 5\hat{j} + 5\hat{k}$

A simpler direction vector parallel to $\vec{b}$ is obtained by dividing by $5$, giving $\vec{d} = \hat{i} - \hat{j} + \hat{k}$.

The equation of the line is given by $\bar{r} = \vec{a} + \lambda \vec{d}$.

$\bar{r} = (\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}) + \lambda(\hat{i} - \hat{j} + \hat{k})$

Answer: $\bar{r}=(\hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k})+\lambda(\hat{i}-\hat{j}+\hat{k})$

</details>

---

### Q98. MHT CET 2024 (02 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

Distance between the parallel lines $\dfrac{x}{3}=\dfrac{y-1}{-2}=\dfrac{z}{1}$ and $\dfrac{x+4}{3}=\dfrac{y-3}{-2}=\dfrac{z+2}{1}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\sqrt{\dfrac{6}{7}}$ units
- **(B)** $\sqrt{\dfrac{3}{7}}$ units
- **(C)** $\sqrt{\dfrac{3}{14}}$ units
- **(D)** $\sqrt{\dfrac{5}{14}}$ units

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\sqrt{\dfrac{6}{7}}$ units**

**Detailed Solution:**

The given lines are parallel to the vector $\vec{b} = 3\hat{i} - 2\hat{j} + \hat{k}$.

A point on the first line is $A(0, 1, 0)$ and a point on the second line is $B(-4, 3, -2)$.

The vector joining these points is $\vec{AB} = -4\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} - 2\hat{k}$.

The distance $d$ between two parallel lines is given by $d = \dfrac{|\vec{AB} \times \vec{b}|}{|\vec{b}|}$.

Calculating the cross product:
$\vec{AB} \times \vec{b} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ -4 & 2 & -2 \\ 3 & -2 & 1 \end{vmatrix} = \hat{i}(2 - 4) - \hat{j}(-4 + 6) + \hat{k}(8 - 6) = -2\hat{i} - 2\hat{j} + 2\hat{k}$

The magnitude of the cross product is:
$|\vec{AB} \times \vec{b}| = \sqrt{(-2)^2 + (-2)^2 + 2^2} = \sqrt{12} = 2\sqrt{3}$

The magnitude of the direction vector $\vec{b}$ is:
$|\vec{b}| = \sqrt{3^2 + (-2)^2 + 1^2} = \sqrt{14}$

Therefore, the distance is:
$d = \dfrac{2\sqrt{3}}{\sqrt{14}} = \sqrt{\dfrac{12}{14}} = \sqrt{\dfrac{6}{7}}$ units

Answer: $\sqrt{\dfrac{6}{7}}$ units

</details>

---

### Q99. MHT CET 2024 (02 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The equation of the line passing through the point $(-1,3,-2)$ and perpendicular to each of the lines $\dfrac{x}{1}=\dfrac{y}{2}=\dfrac{z}{3}$ and $\dfrac{x+2}{-3}=\dfrac{y-1}{2}=\dfrac{z+1}{5}$, is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\dfrac{x+1}{2}=\dfrac{y-3}{7}=\dfrac{z+2}{4}$
- **(B)** $\dfrac{x+1}{-2}=\dfrac{y-3}{-7}=\dfrac{z+2}{4}$
- **(C)** $\dfrac{x+1}{2}=\dfrac{y-3}{7}=\dfrac{z+2}{-4}$
- **(D)** $\dfrac{x+1}{2}=\dfrac{y-3}{-7}=\dfrac{z+2}{4}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\dfrac{x+1}{2}=\dfrac{y-3}{-7}=\dfrac{z+2}{4}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The direction ratios of the given lines are $\vec{b_1} = \hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}$ and $\vec{b_2} = -3\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 5\hat{k}$.

Since the required line is perpendicular to both given lines, its direction vector $\vec{b}$ is parallel to the cross product of $\vec{b_1}$ and $\vec{b_2}$.

$\vec{b} = \vec{b_1} \times \vec{b_2} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1 & 2 & 3 \\ -3 & 2 & 5 \end{vmatrix}$

$\vec{b} = \hat{i}(10 - 6) - \hat{j}(5 - (-9)) + \hat{k}(2 - (-6))$

$\vec{b} = 4\hat{i} - 14\hat{j} + 8\hat{k}$

The direction ratios of the required line are proportional to $(4, -14, 8)$, which simplifies to $(2, -7, 4)$.

The required line passes through the point $(-1, 3, -2)$. Therefore, the equation of the line is:

$\dfrac{x - (-1)}{2} = \dfrac{y - 3}{-7} = \dfrac{z - (-2)}{4}$

$\dfrac{x+1}{2} = \dfrac{y-3}{-7} = \dfrac{z+2}{4}$

Answer: $\dfrac{x+1}{2}=\dfrac{y-3}{-7}=\dfrac{z+2}{4}$

</details>

---

### Q100. MHT CET 2023 (14 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The length of the perpendicular drawn from the point $(1,2,3)$ to the line $\frac{x-6}{3}=\frac{y-7}{2}=\frac{z-7}{-2}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** 4 units
- **(B)** 5 units
- **(C)** 6 units
- **(D)** 7 units

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 7 units**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $\frac{x-6}{3}=\frac{y-7}{2}=\frac{z-7}{-2}=\lambda$ (say)
Any point on the line is $\mathrm{P}(3 \lambda+6,2 \lambda+7,-2 \lambda+7)$
Let $A \equiv(1,2,3)$
The d.r.s. of line AP are
$\begin{aligned}
& 3 \lambda+6-1,2 \lambda+7-2,-2 \lambda+7-3 \\
& \text { i.e. } 3 \lambda+5,2 \lambda+5,-2 \lambda+4
\end{aligned}$
Since AP is perpendicular to the given line,
$\begin{aligned}
& 3(3 \lambda+5)+2(2 \lambda+5)-2(-2 \lambda+4)=0 \\
& \Rightarrow 17 \lambda+17=0 \\
& \Rightarrow \lambda=-1 \\
& \therefore \quad \mathrm{P} \equiv(3,5,9) \\
& \therefore \quad \mathrm{AP}=\sqrt{(3-1)^2+(5-2)^2+(9-3)^2} \\
& \quad=\sqrt{49} \\
& \quad=7 \text { units }
\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q101. MHT CET 2023 (14 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the lines $\frac{x-\mathrm{k}}{2}=\frac{y+1}{3}=\frac{\mathrm{z}-1}{4} \quad$ and $\frac{x-3}{1}=\frac{y-\frac{9}{2}}{2}=\frac{\mathrm{z}}{1}$ intersect, then the value of $\mathrm{k}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac {1}{2}$
- **(B)** $-1$
- **(C)** $1$
- **(D)** $\frac {3}{2}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $1$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Since the given lines intersect
$\begin{aligned}
&\left|\begin{array}{ccc}
x_2-x_1 & y_2-y_1 & \mathrm{z}_2-\mathrm{z}_1 \\
\mathrm{a}_1 & \mathrm{~b}_1 & \mathrm{c}_1 \\
\mathrm{a}_2 & \mathrm{~b}_2 & \mathrm{c}_2
\end{array}\right|=0 \\
& \therefore \quad\left|\begin{array}{ccc}
3-\mathrm{k} & \frac{9}{2}+1 & 0-1 \\
2 & 3 & 4 \\
1 & 2 & 1
\end{array}\right|=0 \\
& \Rightarrow\left|\begin{array}{ccc}
3-\mathrm{k} & \frac{11}{2} & -1 \\
2 & 3 & 4 \\
1 & 2 & 1
\end{array}\right|=0 \\
& \Rightarrow-5+5 \mathrm{k}=0 \\
& \Rightarrow \mathrm{k}=1
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q102. MHT CET 2023 (14 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Consider the lines
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{L}_1: \frac{x+1}{3}=\frac{y+2}{1}=\frac{\mathrm{z}+1}{2} \\
& \mathrm{~L}_2: \frac{x-2}{1}=\frac{y+2}{2}=\frac{z-3}{3}
\end{aligned}
$$
then the unit vector perpendicular to both $\mathrm{L}_1$ and $\mathrm{L}_2$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{-\hat{\mathrm{i}}+7 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+5 \hat{\mathrm{k}}}{5 \sqrt{3}}$
- **(B)** $\frac{-\hat{\mathrm{i}}-7 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+5 \hat{\mathrm{k}}}{5 \sqrt{3}}$
- **(C)** $\frac{+\hat{\mathrm{i}}-7 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+5 \hat{\mathrm{k}}}{5 \sqrt{3}}$
- **(D)** $\frac{\hat{\mathrm{i}}+7 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+5 \hat{\mathrm{k}}}{5 \sqrt{3}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\frac{-\hat{\mathrm{i}}-7 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+5 \hat{\mathrm{k}}}{5 \sqrt{3}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Lines $L_1$ and $L_2$ are parallel to the vectors
$$
\overline{\mathrm{b}}_1=3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{k}} \text { and } \overline{\mathrm{b}}_2=\hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{k}}
$$
respectively.
$\therefore$ The unit vector perpendicular to both $\mathrm{L}_1$ and $\mathrm{L}_2$ is $\hat{\mathrm{n}}=\frac{\overline{\mathrm{b}}_1 \times \overline{\mathrm{b}}_2}{\left|\overline{\mathrm{b}}_1 \times \overline{\mathrm{b}}_2\right|}$
Now, $\bar{b}_1 \times \bar{b}_2=\left|\begin{array}{lll}\hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 3 & 1 & 2 \\ 1 & 2 & 3\end{array}\right|=-\hat{\mathrm{i}}-7 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+5 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$
$\therefore \quad \hat{\mathrm{n}}=\frac{1}{5 \sqrt{3}}(-\hat{\mathrm{i}}-7 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+5 \hat{\mathrm{k}})$

</details>

---

### Q103. MHT CET 2023 (14 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the shortest distance between the lines $\frac{x-1}{2}=\frac{y-2}{3}=\frac{z-3}{\lambda}$ and $\frac{x-2}{1}=\frac{y-4}{4}=\frac{z-5}{5}$ is $\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}$, then sum of possible values of $\lambda$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $16$
- **(B)** $11$
- **(C)** $12$
- **(D)** $15$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $16$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The shortest distance between the lines $\frac{x-1}{2}=\frac{y-2}{3}=\frac{z-3}{\lambda}$ and $\frac{x-2}{1}=\frac{y-4}{4}=\frac{z-5}{5}$ is $\mathrm{d}=\left|\frac{\left|\begin{array}{ccc}x_2-x_1 & y_2-y_1 & \mathrm{z}_2-\mathrm{z}_1 \\ \mathrm{a}_1 & \mathrm{~b}_1 & \mathrm{c}_1 \\ \mathrm{a}_2 & \mathrm{~b}_2 & \mathrm{c}_2\end{array}\right|}{\sqrt{\left(\mathrm{a}_1 \mathrm{~b}_2-\mathrm{a}_2 \mathrm{~b}_1\right)^2+\left(\mathrm{a}_1 \mathrm{c}_2-\mathrm{a}_2 \mathrm{c}_1\right)^2+\left(\mathrm{b}_1 \mathrm{c}_2-\mathrm{b}_2 \mathrm{c}_1\right)^2}}\right|$
$\therefore \quad d=\left|\frac{\left|\begin{array}{lll}1 & 2 & 2 \\ 2 & 3 & \lambda \\ 1 & 4 & 5\end{array}\right|}{\sqrt{(8-3)^2+(10-\lambda)^2+(15-4 \lambda)^2}}\right|$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \Rightarrow \frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}=\left|\frac{5-2 \lambda}{\sqrt{17 \lambda^2-140 \lambda+350}}\right| \\
& \Rightarrow \frac{1}{3}=\frac{25-20 \lambda+4 \lambda^2}{17 \lambda^2-140 \lambda+350} \\
& \Rightarrow 5 \lambda^2-80 \lambda+275=0 \\
& \Rightarrow \lambda^2-16 \lambda+55=0 \\
& \Rightarrow(\lambda-5)(\lambda-11)=0 \\
& \Rightarrow \lambda=5 \text { or } \lambda=11
\end{aligned}
$$
$\therefore \quad$ Sum of possible values of $\lambda=16$

</details>

---

### Q104. MHT CET 2023 (13 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The acute angle between the line joining the points $(2,1,-3),(-3,1,7)$ and a line parallel to $\frac{x-1}{3}=\frac{y}{4}=\frac{z+3}{5}$ through the point $(-1,0,4)$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{10}}\right)$
- **(B)** $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{5}{7 \sqrt{10}}\right)$
- **(C)** $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{7}{5 \sqrt{10}}\right)$
- **(D)** $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{3}{5 \sqrt{10}}\right)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{7}{5 \sqrt{10}}\right)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The d.r.s. of the line joining the points $(2,1,-3)$ and $(-3,1,7)$ are $-5,0,10$
The d.r.s. of the line parallel to line $\frac{x-1}{3}=\frac{y}{4}=\frac{z+3}{5}$ are $3,4,5$
$\therefore \quad$ The angle between the lines having d.r.s.
$-5,0,10$ and $3,4,5$ is
$\cos \theta=\left|\frac{-5(3)+0(4)+10(5)}{\sqrt{25+0+100} \sqrt{9+16+25}}\right|$
$\begin{aligned} & \Rightarrow \cos \theta=\frac{35}{25 \sqrt{10}} \\ & \Rightarrow \theta=\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{7}{5 \sqrt{10}}\right)\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q105. MHT CET 2023 (13 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The foot of the perpendicular from the point $(1,2,3)$ on the line $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=(6 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+7 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+7 \hat{\mathrm{k}})+\lambda(3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-2 \hat{\mathrm{k}})$ has the co-ordinates

**Options:**

- **(A)** $(3,5,9)$
- **(B)** $(5,-3,9)$
- **(C)** $(3,-5,-9)$
- **(D)** $(5,-9,3)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $(3,5,9)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $\frac{x-6}{3}=\frac{y-7}{2}=\frac{z-7}{-2}=\lambda$
Any point on the line is
$\mathrm{P} \equiv(3 \lambda+6,2 \lambda+7,-2 \lambda+7)$
Given point is $\mathrm{A}(1,2,3)$
$\therefore \quad$ The d.r.s. of the line AP are $3 \lambda+5,2 \lambda+5$, $-2 \lambda+4$
Since the line AP is perpendicular to the given line.
$\therefore \quad 3(3 \lambda+5)+2(2 \lambda+5)-2(-2 \lambda+4)=0$
$\begin{aligned} & \Rightarrow 17 \lambda+17=0 \\ & \Rightarrow \lambda=-1\end{aligned}$
$\therefore \quad \mathrm{P} \equiv(3,5,9)$

</details>

### Q106. MHT CET 2023 (13 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The equation of the line passing through the point $(-1,3,-2)$ and perpendicular to each of the lines $\frac{x}{1}=\frac{y}{2}=\frac{z}{3}$ and $\frac{x+2}{-3}=\frac{y-1}{2}=\frac{z+1}{5}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{x+1}{2}=\frac{y-3}{7}=\frac{z+2}{4}$
- **(B)** $\frac{x+1}{2}=\frac{y-3}{-7}=\frac{z+2}{4}$
- **(C)** $\frac{x-1}{2}=\frac{y+3}{-7}=\frac{z+2}{4}$
- **(D)** $\frac{x-1}{2}=\frac{y+3}{7}=\frac{z-2}{4}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\frac{x+1}{2}=\frac{y-3}{-7}=\frac{z+2}{4}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $\mathrm{a}, \mathrm{b}, \mathrm{c}$ be the direction ratios of the required line. Since the line is perpendicular to the lines with d.r.s. $1,2,3$ and $-3,2,5$.
$$
\begin{aligned}
\therefore \quad & a+2 b+3 c=0 \\
& \text { and }-3 a+2 b+5 c=0 . \\
& \Rightarrow \frac{a}{2}=\frac{b}{-7}=\frac{c}{4}
\end{aligned}
$$
....[From (i) and (ii)]
$\therefore \quad$ Equation of the required line is
$$
\frac{x+1}{2}=\frac{y-3}{-7}=\frac{z+2}{4}
$$

</details>

### Q107. MHT CET 2023 (13 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A line $\mathrm{L}_1$ passes through the point, whose p. v. (position vector) $3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}$, is parallel to the vector $-\hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}$. Another line $\mathrm{L}_2$ passes through the point having p.v. $\hat{i}+\hat{j}$ is parallel to vector $\hat{i}+\hat{k}$, then the point of intersection of lines $L_1$ and $L_2$ has p.v.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2 \hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+\hat{k}$
- **(B)** $2 \hat{i}+\hat{j}+\hat{k}$
- **(C)** $2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-\hat{\mathrm{j}}-\hat{\mathrm{k}}$
- **(D)** $2 \hat{i}-2 \hat{j}+\hat{k}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $2 \hat{i}+\hat{j}+\hat{k}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Equation of line $\mathrm{L}_1$ is $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\lambda(-\hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}})$
Equation of line $L_2$ is $\overline{r^{\prime}}=\hat{i}+\hat{j}+\lambda^{\prime}(\hat{i}+\hat{k})$
The point of intersection of $\mathrm{L}_1$ and $\mathrm{L}_2$ will satisfy $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=\overline{\mathrm{r}^{\prime}}$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \Rightarrow 3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\lambda(-\hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}})=\hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}+\lambda^{\prime}(\hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}) \\
& \Rightarrow(3-\lambda) \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\lambda \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\lambda \hat{\mathrm{k}}=\left(1+\lambda^{\prime}\right) \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}+\lambda^{\prime} \hat{\mathrm{k}} \\
& \Rightarrow 3-\lambda=1+\lambda^{\prime} \text { and } \lambda=1 \\
& \Rightarrow \lambda=1 \text { and } \lambda^{\prime}=1
\end{aligned}
$$
Substituting the value of $\lambda$ in (i), we get the point of intersection.
$\therefore \quad$ The point of intersection of lines $\mathrm{L}_1$ and $\mathrm{L}_2$ has p.v. $2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}$.

</details>

---

### Q108. MHT CET 2023 (12 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The shortest distance (in units) between the lines $\frac{x+1}{3}=\frac{y+2}{1}=\frac{z+1}{2}$ and $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=(2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{k}})+\lambda(\hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}})$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{8}{3 \sqrt{5}}$
- **(B)** $\frac{1}{3 \sqrt{5}}$
- **(C)** $\frac{7}{3 \sqrt{5}}$
- **(D)** $\frac{2}{3 \sqrt{5}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\frac{8}{3 \sqrt{5}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given lines are: $\frac{x+1}{3}=\frac{y+2}{1}=\frac{z+1}{2}$ and $\frac{x-2}{1}=\frac{y+2}{2}=\frac{z-3}{0}$
$\therefore \quad$ Required distance
$$
=\mid \frac{\left|\begin{array}{lll}
3 & 0 & 4 \\
3 & 1 & 2 \\
1 & 2 & 0
\end{array}\right|}{\sqrt{(6-1)^2+(0-2)^2+(0-4)^2}}
$$
$\begin{aligned} & =\left|\frac{3(0-4)+0+4(6-1)}{\sqrt{25+4+16}}\right| \\ & =\left|\frac{8}{\sqrt{45}}\right| \\ & =\frac{8}{3 \sqrt{5}}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q109. MHT CET 2023 (12 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The equation of the line, passing through $(1,2,3)$ and parallel to planes $x-y+2 z=5$ and $3 x+y+z=6$, is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{x-1}{-3}=\frac{y-2}{5}=\frac{z-3}{4}$
- **(B)** $\frac{x-1}{-3}=\frac{y-2}{-5}=\frac{z-3}{4}$
- **(C)** $\frac{x-1}{4}=\frac{y-2}{5}=\frac{z-3}{3}$
- **(D)** $\frac{x-1}{5}=\frac{y-2}{7}=\frac{z-3}{1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\frac{x-1}{-3}=\frac{y-2}{5}=\frac{z-3}{4}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Required equation of line is
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \frac{x-1}{\left|\begin{array}{cc}
-1 & 2 \\
1 & 1
\end{array}\right|}=\frac{y-2}{-\left|\begin{array}{ll}
1 & 2 \\
3 & 1
\end{array}\right|}=\frac{\mathrm{z}-3}{\left|\begin{array}{cc}
1 & -1 \\
3 & 1
\end{array}\right|} \\
\therefore \quad & \frac{x-1}{-3}=\frac{y-2}{5}=\frac{z-3}{4}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q110. MHT CET 2023 (12 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the line $\frac{1-x}{3}=\frac{7 y-14}{2 p}=\frac{z-3}{2}$ and $\frac{7-7 x}{3 p}=\frac{y-5}{1}=\frac{6-z}{5}$ are at right angles, then $p=$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{70}{11}$
- **(B)** $\frac{11}{70}$
- **(C)** $\frac{-70}{11}$
- **(D)** $\frac{-11}{70}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\frac{70}{11}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given lines can be written as
$$
\frac{x-1}{-3}=\frac{y-2}{\frac{2 p}{7}}=\frac{z-3}{2} \text { and } \frac{x-1}{3 p}=\frac{y-5}{1}=\frac{z-6}{-5}
$$
As these lines are at right angles, we get
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \quad-(-3)\left(-\frac{3 p}{7}\right)+\left(\frac{2 p}{7}\right)(1)+(2)(-5)=0 \\
& \therefore \quad \frac{9 p}{7}+\frac{2 p}{7}-10=0 \\
& \therefore \quad \frac{11 p}{7}=10 \Rightarrow p=\frac{70}{11}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q111. MHT CET 2023 (12 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The distance of the point $\mathrm{P}(-2,4,-5)$ from the line $\frac{x+3}{3}=\frac{y-4}{5}=\frac{z+8}{6}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{\sqrt{37}}{10}$
- **(B)** $\sqrt{\frac{37}{10}}$
- **(C)** $\frac{37}{\sqrt{10}}$
- **(D)** $\frac{37}{10}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\sqrt{\frac{37}{10}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Since the point is $(-2,4,-5)$,
$$
\therefore \quad \mathrm{a}=-2, \mathrm{~b}=4, \mathrm{c}=-5
$$
Given equation of line is
$$
\frac{x+3}{3}=\frac{y-4}{5}=\frac{z+8}{6}
$$
$$
\therefore \quad x_1=-3, y_1=4, z_1=-8
$$
d.r.s of the line are $3,5,6$
$\therefore \quad$ d.c.s are $\frac{3}{\sqrt{70}}, \frac{5}{\sqrt{70}}, \frac{6}{\sqrt{70}}$
Perpendicular distance of point from the line is
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \sqrt{\begin{array}{r}
{\left[\left(\mathrm{a}-x_1\right)^2+\left(\mathrm{b}-y_1\right)^2+\left(\mathrm{c}-\mathrm{z}_1\right)^2\right]} \\
-\left[\left(\mathrm{a}-x_1\right) l+\left(\mathrm{b}-y_1\right) \mathrm{m}+\left(\mathrm{c}-\mathrm{z}_1\right) \mathrm{n}\right]^2
\end{array}} \\
= & \sqrt{1^2+0+3^2-\left[\frac{1(3)}{\sqrt{70}}+\frac{0(5)}{\sqrt{70}}+\frac{3(6)}{\sqrt{70}}\right]^2} \\
= & \sqrt{1+9-\left(\frac{3}{\sqrt{70}}+\frac{18}{\sqrt{70}}\right)^2} \\
= & \sqrt{\frac{37}{10}} \text { units }
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

---

### Q112. MHT CET 2023 (11 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The equation of line passing through the point $(1,2,3)$ and perpendicular to the lines $\frac{x-2}{3}=\frac{y-1}{2}=\frac{z+1}{-2}$ and $\frac{x}{2}=\frac{y}{-3}=\frac{z}{1}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=(\hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{k}})+\lambda(4 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+7 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-13 \hat{\mathrm{k}})$
- **(B)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=(\hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{k}})+\lambda(-4 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+7 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-13 \hat{\mathrm{k}})$
- **(C)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=(\hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{k}})+\lambda(-4 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-7 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-13 \hat{\mathrm{k}})$
- **(D)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=(\hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{k}})+\lambda(4 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-7 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-13 \hat{\mathrm{k}})$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=(\hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{k}})+\lambda(-4 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-7 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-13 \hat{\mathrm{k}})$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Required line is perpendicular to the lines $\frac{x-2}{3}=\frac{y-1}{2}=\frac{z+1}{-2}$ and $\frac{x}{2}=\frac{y}{-3}=\frac{z}{1}$
$\therefore \quad$ Required line is parallel to vector
$\overline{\mathrm{b}}=\left|\begin{array}{ccc}
\hat{\mathrm{i}} & \hat{\mathrm{j}} & \hat{\mathrm{k}} \\
3 & 2 & -2 \\
2 & -3 & 1
\end{array}\right|=-4 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-7 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-13 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$
$\therefore \quad$ The equation of the required line is
$(\hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k})+\lambda(-4 \hat{i}-7 \hat{j}-13 \hat{k})$

</details>

### Q113. MHT CET 2023 (11 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If the lines $\frac{x-1}{2}=\frac{y+1}{3}=\frac{z-1}{4} \quad$ and $x-3=\frac{y-\mathrm{k}}{2}=\mathrm{z}$ intersect, then the value of $\mathrm{k}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{3}{2}$
- **(B)** $\frac{-2}{9}$
- **(C)** $\frac{-2}{3}$
- **(D)** $\frac{9}{2}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\frac{9}{2}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

As the given lines are intersecting, the shortest distance between them is zero.
$\begin{array}{ll}
\therefore & \left|\begin{array}{ccc}
x_2-x_1 & y_2-y_1 & \mathrm{z}_2-\mathrm{z}_1 \\
\mathrm{a}_1 & \mathrm{~b}_1 & \mathrm{c}_1 \\
\mathrm{a}_2 & \mathrm{~b}_2 & \mathrm{c}_2
\end{array}\right|=0 \\
\therefore & \left|\begin{array}{ccc}
3-1 & \mathrm{k}+1 & 0-1 \\
2 & 3 & 4 \\
1 & 2 & 1
\end{array}\right|=0 \\
\therefore & \left|\begin{array}{ccc}
2 & \mathrm{k}+1 & -1 \\
2 & 3 & 4 \\
1 & 2 & 1
\end{array}\right|=0 \\
\therefore & \mathrm{k}=\frac{9}{2}
\end{array}$

</details>

### Q114. MHT CET 2023 (11 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The vector equation of the line $2 x+4=3 y+1=6 z-3$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=\left(2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\frac{1}{3} \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\frac{1}{2} \hat{\mathrm{k}}\right)+\lambda(3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}})$
- **(B)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=\left(-2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-\frac{1}{3} \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\frac{1}{2} \hat{\mathrm{k}}\right)+\lambda(3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}})$
- **(C)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=(2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}})+\lambda(3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}})$
- **(D)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=(-2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-\hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}})+\lambda(3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}})$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=\left(-2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-\frac{1}{3} \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\frac{1}{2} \hat{\mathrm{k}}\right)+\lambda(3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}})$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equation of line is
$$
\begin{aligned}
& 2 x+4=3 y+1=6 z-3 \\
& \Rightarrow 2(x+2)=3\left(y+\frac{1}{3}\right)=6\left(z-\frac{1}{2}\right) \\
& \Rightarrow \frac{x+2}{\frac{1}{2}}=\frac{y+\frac{1}{3}}{\frac{1}{3}}=\frac{z-\frac{1}{2}}{\frac{1}{6}} \\
& \Rightarrow \frac{x+2}{3}=\frac{y+\frac{1}{3}}{2}=\frac{z-\frac{1}{2}}{1}
\end{aligned}
$$
$\therefore \quad$ The given line passes through $\left(-2,-\frac{1}{3}, \frac{1}{2}\right)$ and has direction ratios proportional to $3,2,1$.
$\therefore \quad$ Vector equation of the line is
$$
\overline{\mathrm{r}}=\left(-2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-\frac{1}{3} \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\frac{1}{2} \hat{\mathrm{k}}\right)+\lambda(3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}})
$$

</details>

---

### Q115. MHT CET 2023 (11 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The lines $\frac{x-1}{3}=\frac{y+1}{2}=\frac{z-1}{5} \quad$ and $\frac{x+2}{4}=\frac{y-1}{3}=\frac{z+1}{2}$

**Options:**

- **(A)** intersect each other and point of intersection is $(2,1,3)$
- **(B)** intersect each other and point of intersection is $(3,2,4)$
- **(C)** intersect each other and point of intersection is $(-2,3,3)$
- **(D)** do not intersect.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) do not intersect.**

**Detailed Solution:**

The given lines are $\frac{x-1}{3}=\frac{y+1}{2}=\frac{z-1}{5}$ and $\frac{x+2}{4}=\frac{y-1}{3}=\frac{z+1}{2}$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \text { Here, } \\
& \qquad\left(x_1, y_1, \mathrm{z}_1\right) \equiv(1,-1,1) \\
& \qquad\left(x_2, y_2, \mathrm{z}_2\right) \equiv(-2,1,-1) \\
& \qquad\left(\mathrm{a}_1, \mathrm{~b}_1, \mathrm{c}_1\right) \equiv(3,2,5) \\
& \text { Consider }\left|\begin{array}{ccc}
x_2-x_1 & y_2-y_1 & \mathrm{z}_2-\mathrm{z}_1 \\
\mathrm{a}_1 & \mathrm{~b}_1 & \mathrm{c}_1 \\
\mathrm{a}_2 & \mathrm{~b}_2 & \mathrm{c}_2
\end{array}\right| \\
& =\left|\begin{array}{ccc}
-3 & 2 & -2 \\
3 & 2 & 5 \\
4 & 3 & 2
\end{array}\right| \\
& =-3(-11)-2(-14)-2(1) \\
& =33+28-2 \\
& =59 \neq 0
\end{aligned}
$$
$\therefore \quad$ The lines are not intersecting.

</details>

---

### Q116. MHT CET 2023 (09 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the Cartesian equation of a line is $6 x-2=3 y+1=2 z-2$, then the vector equation of the line is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=\left(\frac{1}{3} \hat{\mathrm{i}}-\frac{1}{3} \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}\right)+\lambda(\hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{k}})$
- **(B)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=(\hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}})+\lambda(\hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{k}})$
- **(C)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=\left(\frac{-1}{3} \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\frac{1}{3} \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}\right)+\lambda(\hat{\mathrm{i}}-2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{k}})$
- **(D)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=\left(\frac{1}{3} \hat{\mathrm{i}}-\frac{1}{3} \hat{\mathrm{j}}-\hat{\mathrm{k}}\right)+\lambda(\hat{\mathrm{i}}-\hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}})$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=\left(\frac{1}{3} \hat{\mathrm{i}}-\frac{1}{3} \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}\right)+\lambda(\hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{k}})$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given Cartesian equation of the line is
$\begin{aligned}
& 6 x-2=3 y+1=2 z-2 \\
& \Rightarrow 6\left(x-\frac{1}{3}\right)=3\left(y+\frac{1}{3}\right)=2(z-1)
\end{aligned}$
$\begin{aligned}
& \Rightarrow \frac{x-\frac{1}{3}}{\frac{1}{6}}=\frac{y+\frac{1}{3}}{\frac{1}{3}}=\frac{z-1}{\frac{1}{2}} \\
& \Rightarrow \frac{x-\frac{1}{3}}{1}=\frac{y+\frac{1}{3}}{2}=\frac{z-1}{3}
\end{aligned}$
$\therefore \quad$ The given line passes through $\left(\frac{1}{3}, \frac{-1}{3}, 1\right)$ and has direction ratios proportional to $1,2,3$.
$\therefore \quad$ Vector equation is
$\overline{\mathrm{r}}=\left(\frac{1}{3} \hat{\mathrm{i}}-\frac{1}{3} \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}\right)+\lambda(\hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{k}})$

</details>

---

### Q117. MHT CET 2023 (09 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The distance of the point having position vector $\hat{i}-2 \hat{j}-6 \hat{k}$, from the straight line passing through the point $(2,-3,-4)$ and parallel to the vector $6 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-4 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$ is units.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\sqrt{\frac{340}{61}}$
- **(B)** $\frac{341}{61}$
- **(C)** $\frac{\sqrt {341}}{61}$
- **(D)** $\sqrt{\frac{341}{61}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\sqrt{\frac{341}{61}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given equation of the line is
$\begin{aligned}
& \overline{\mathrm{r}}=\overline{\mathrm{a}}+\lambda \overline{\mathrm{b}} \\
& \therefore \quad \bar{r}=2 \hat{i}-3 \hat{j}-4 \hat{k}+\lambda(6 \hat{i}+3 \hat{j}-4 \hat{k}) \\
&
\end{aligned}$
To find: Its distance from point $\bar{\alpha}=\hat{i}-2 \hat{j}-6 \hat{k}$.
$\therefore \quad \text { Required distance }=\sqrt{|\bar{\alpha}-\bar{a}|^2-\left[\frac{(\bar{\alpha}-\bar{a}) \cdot \bar{b}}{|\bar{b}|}\right]^2}$
Here, $|\bar{\alpha}-\bar{a}|^2=6$ and $\left[\frac{(\bar{\alpha}-\bar{a}) \cdot \bar{b}}{|\bar{b}|}\right]^2=\frac{25}{61}$.
$\therefore \quad \text { Requiređ distance }=\sqrt{\frac{341}{61}}$

</details>

---

### Q118. MHT CET 2022 (11 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The shortest distance between the lines $\frac{x-3}{3}=\frac{y-8}{-1}=\frac{z-3}{1}$ and $\frac{x+3}{-3}=\frac{y+7}{2}=\frac{z-6}{4}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2 \sqrt{30}$ units
- **(B)** $\sqrt{30}$ units
- **(C)** $4 \sqrt{30}$ units
- **(D)** $3 \sqrt{30}$ units

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $3 \sqrt{30}$ units**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \text { Shortest distance }=\left|\frac{\left|\begin{array}{ccc}3-(-3) & 8-(-7) & 3-6 \\ 3 & -1 & 1 \\ -3 & 2 & 4\end{array}\right|}{\sqrt{(-4-2)^2+(12+3)^2+(6-3)^2}}\right| \\ & =\frac{|6 \times(-6)-15 \times(15)-3 \times(3)|}{\sqrt{36+225+9}} \\ & =\frac{270}{\sqrt{270}}=\sqrt{270}=3 \sqrt{30}\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q119. MHT CET 2022 (10 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The length of the perpendicular from the point $(0,2,3)$ on the line $\frac{x+3}{5}=\frac{y-1}{2}=\frac{z+4}{3}$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\sqrt{15}$ units
- **(B)** $\sqrt{21}$ units
- **(C)** $\sqrt{33}$ units
- **(D)** $\sqrt{11}$ units

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\sqrt{21}$ units**

**Detailed Solution:**

Any point on the line $\frac{x+3}{5}=\frac{y-1}{2}=\frac{z+4}{3}=\lambda$ can be taken as
$$
(5 \lambda-3,2 \lambda+1,3 \lambda-4)
$$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \text { for foot of perpendicular } \lambda=\frac{a\left(\alpha-x_1\right)+b\left(\beta-y_1\right)+c\left(\gamma-z_1\right)}{a^2+b^2+c^2} \\
& =\frac{5(0+3)+2(2-1)+3(3+4)}{5^2+2^2+3^2} \\
& \text { i.e., } \lambda=1 \\
& \Rightarrow \text { foot of perpendicular }(2,3,-1) \\
& \Rightarrow \text { Length of the perpendicular }=\sqrt{(0-2)^2+(2-3)^2+(3+1)^2} \\
& =\sqrt{21}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

---

### Q120. MHT CET 2022 (10 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The Cartesian equation of the line which passes through the points $(3,1,2)$ and $(-1,2,1)$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{x-3}{-4}=\frac{y-1}{1}=\frac{z-2}{1}$
- **(B)** $\frac{x-3}{-4}=\frac{y-1}{1}=\frac{z-2}{-1}$
- **(C)** $\frac{x-3}{-4}=\frac{y-1}{-1}=\frac{z-2}{-1}$
- **(D)** $\frac{x-3}{-4}=\frac{y-1}{-1}=\frac{z-2}{1}$

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\frac{x-3}{-4}=\frac{y-1}{1}=\frac{z-2}{-1}$**

The required equation of the line is
$\begin{aligned} & \frac{x-3}{-1-3}=\frac{y-1}{2-1}=\frac{z-2}{1-2} \\ & \Rightarrow \frac{x-3}{-4}=\frac{y-1}{1}=\frac{z-2}{-1}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q121. MHT CET 2022 (10 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the foot of the perpendicular drawn from the origin to a plane is $M(-1,-2,2)$, then the vector equation of the plane is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\bar{r} \cdot(-\hat{i}-2 \hat{j}+2 \widehat{k})=9$
- **(B)** $\bar{r} \cdot(\widehat{i}+2 \widehat{j}+2 \widehat{k})=9$
- **(C)** $\bar{r} \cdot(-\hat{i}-2 \hat{j}-2 \widehat{k})=9$
- **(D)** $\bar{r} \cdot(\hat{i}+2 \hat{j}-2 \widehat{k})=9$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\bar{r} \cdot(-\hat{i}-2 \hat{j}+2 \widehat{k})=9$**

**Detailed Solution:**

D.r's of normal to the plane $<-1,-2,2>$
D.c's of Normal to the plane $<-\frac{1}{3},-\frac{2}{3}, \frac{2}{3}>$
length of perpendicular $=\sqrt{1^2+2^2+(-2)^2}=3$
Hence, Equation of plane $\vec{r} \cdot\left(-\frac{1}{3} \hat{i}-\frac{2}{3} \hat{j}+\frac{2}{3} \hat{k}\right)=3$
$\Rightarrow \vec{r} \cdot(-\hat{i}-2 \hat{j}+2 \widehat{k})=9$

</details>

### Q122. MHT CET 2022 (08 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Then foot of the perpendicular from the point $(0,2,3)$ on the line $\frac{x+3}{5}=\frac{y-1}{2}=\frac{z+4}{3}$

**Options:**

- **(A)** (2,3,1)
- **(B)** (2,3,-1)
- **(C)** (2,-3,1)
- **(D)** (-2,3,1)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) (2,3,-1)**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/mht_cet/_cuuxTs1J8z4SOORBx_0lSG4_mVighZ3FleGrpytYyo.original.fullsize.png">

Any point on the line $\frac{x+3}{5}=\frac{y-1}{3}=\frac{z+4}{3}=\lambda$ can be taken as $(5 \lambda-3,2 \lambda+1,3 \lambda-4)$
for foot of perpendicular $\lambda=\frac{a\left(\alpha-x_1\right)+b\left(\beta-y_1\right)+c\left(\gamma-z_1\right)}{a^2+b^2+c^2}$
$\begin{aligned}
& \Rightarrow \lambda=\frac{5(0+3)+2(2-1)+3(3+4)}{5^2+2^2+3^2} \\
& \Rightarrow \lambda=\frac{38}{38}=1
\end{aligned}$
Hence foot of perpendicular is
$(5 \times 1-3,2 \times 1+1,3 \times 1-4) \equiv(2,3,-1)$

</details>

### Q123. MHT CET 2022 (08 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The line $\frac{x-1}{3}=\frac{y+1}{2}=\frac{z-1}{5}$ and $\frac{x+2}{4}=\frac{y-1}{3}=\frac{z+1}{-2}$

**Options:**

- **(A)** intersect each other and point of intersection is $(4,3,-2)$.
- **(B)** do not intersect.
- **(C)** intersect each other and point of intersection is $(3,2,5)$.
- **(D)** intersect each other and point of intersection is $(-2,-1,-1)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) do not intersect.**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned}
& \text { S.D. }=\left|\frac{\left|\begin{array}{ccc}
1+2 & -1-1 & 1+1 \\
3 & 2 & 5 \\
4 & 3 & -2
\end{array}\right|}{\sqrt{(-4-15)^2+(20+6)^2+(9-8)^2}}\right| \\
& =\left|\frac{3(-4-15)-2(20+6)+2(9-8)}{\sqrt{19^2+26^2+1^2}}\right| \\
& =\frac{|-57-52+2|}{\sqrt{361+676+1}}=\frac{107}{\sqrt{1039}} \neq 0
\end{aligned}$
Hence, the two lines do not intersect each other.

</details>

### Q124. MHT CET 2022 (08 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The Cartesian equation of a line is $\frac{x+2}{3}=\frac{y-4}{2}=\frac{z-5}{5}$, then the vector equation of the line is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\bar{r}=(-2 \hat{i}+4 \hat{j}+5 \hat{k})+\lambda(3 \hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+5 \hat{k})$
- **(B)** $\bar{r}=(2 \hat{i}-4 \hat{j}-5 \hat{k})+\lambda(-3 \hat{i}+2 \hat{j}-5 \hat{k})$
- **(C)** $\bar{r}=(-2 \hat{i}+4 \hat{j}+5 \hat{k})+\lambda(10 \hat{i}+25 \hat{j}-16 \hat{k})$
- **(D)** $\bar{r}=(3 \hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+5 \hat{k})+\lambda(10 \hat{i}+25 \hat{j}-16 \hat{k})$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\bar{r}=(2 \hat{i}-4 \hat{j}-5 \hat{k})+\lambda(-3 \hat{i}+2 \hat{j}-5 \hat{k})$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The line $\frac{x+2}{3}=\frac{y-4}{2}=\frac{z-5}{5}$ is passing through $(-2,4,5)$ and has d.r's $ < 3,2,5>$
Hence, the vector equation is
$\vec{r}=(-2 \hat{i}+4 \hat{j}+5 \hat{k})+\lambda(3 \hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+5 \hat{k})$

</details>

### Q125. MHT CET 2022 (08 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The distance between the lines $\frac{x}{2}=\frac{y}{-1}=\frac{z}{2}$ and $\frac{x-1}{2}=\frac{y-1}{-1}=\frac{z-2}{2}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\sqrt{3}$ units
- **(B)** $\sqrt{2}$ units
- **(C)** 1 unit
- **(D)** 2 units

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\sqrt{2}$ units**

**Detailed Solution:**

Distance between the liens $=\frac{\left|\begin{array}{ccc}\hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1-0 & 1-0 & 1-0 \\ 2 & -1 & 2\end{array}\right|}{\sqrt{2^2+(-1)^2+2^2}}$
$=\frac{|3 \hat{i}-3 \hat{k}|}{\sqrt{9}}=\frac{\sqrt{3^2+3^2}}{\sqrt{3}}=\sqrt{2}$

</details>

---

### Q126. MHT CET 2022 (07 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The vector equation of plane containing the pint $(1,-1,2)$ and perpendicular to planes $2 x+3 y-2 z=5$ and $x+2 y-3 z=8$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\vec{r} \cdot(-5 \hat{i}+4 \hat{j}+\hat{k})=7$
- **(B)** $\vec{r} \cdot(-5 \hat{i}+4 \hat{j}-\hat{k})=-7$
- **(C)** $\vec{r} \cdot(-5 \hat{i}+4 \hat{j}+\hat{k})=-7$
- **(D)** $\vec{r} \cdot(-5 \hat{i}+4 \hat{j}-\widehat{k})=7$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\vec{r} \cdot(-5 \hat{i}+4 \hat{j}+\hat{k})=-7$**

**Detailed Solution:**

D.R's of the required plane can be obtained by
$\begin{aligned} & \frac{a}{3 \times(-3)-2 \times(-2)}=\frac{b}{1 \times(-2)-2 \times(-3)}=\frac{c}{2 \times 2-1 \times 3} \\ & \Rightarrow \frac{a}{-5}=\frac{b}{4}=\frac{c}{1}\end{aligned}$
$\Rightarrow$ the required equation is
$\begin{aligned} & -5(x-1)+4(y+1)+1(z-2)=0 \\ & \Rightarrow-5 x+4 y+z=-7 \\ & \Rightarrow \vec{r} \cdot(-5 \hat{i}+4 \hat{j}+\hat{k})=-7\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q127. MHT CET 2022 (07 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The angle between the lines $\bar{r}=(\hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k})+\lambda(2 \hat{i}-2 \hat{i}+\hat{k})$. and $\bar{r}=(\hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k})+\lambda(2 \hat{i}+2 \hat{i}+\hat{k}) \hat{i}+2 j+k$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $90^{\circ}$
- **(B)** $0^{\circ}$
- **(C)** $30^{\circ}$
- **(D)** $60^{\circ}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $90^{\circ}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\theta=\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{2 \times 1-2 \times 2+1 \times 2}{\sqrt{2^2+2^2+1^2} \sqrt{1^2+2^2+2^2}}\right)=\cos ^{-1}(0)=90^{\circ}$

</details>

### Q128. MHT CET 2022 (07 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the position vectors of the point $\mathrm{A}$ and $\mathrm{B}$ are $3 \hat{i}+\hat{j}+2 \hat{k}$ and $\hat{i}-2 \hat{j}-4 \hat{k}$ respectively, then the equation of the plane through $\mathrm{B}$ and perpendicular to $\mathrm{AB}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2 x+3 y+6 z+9=0$
- **(B)** $2 x+3 y+6 z-11=0$
- **(C)** $2 x+3 y+6 z+28=0$
- **(D)** $2 x-3 y-6 z-32=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $2 x+3 y+6 z+28=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The normal to the plane is along $\overrightarrow{\mathrm{AB}}$
$\begin{aligned} & =-(\hat{i}-2 \hat{j}-4 \hat{k})-(3 \hat{i}+\hat{j}+2 \hat{k}) \\ & =-2 \hat{i}-3 \hat{j}-6 \hat{k}\end{aligned}$
Hence, d.r.s of normal to the plane are $<2,3,6>$
so equation of the plane
$2 x+3 y+6 z+\lambda=0$
but it passes through $(1,-2,-4)$, so $\lambda=28$
$\Rightarrow 2 x+3 y+6 z+28=0$

</details>

### Q129. MHT CET 2022 (07 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If the line passing through the points $(a, 1,6)$ and $(3,4, b)$ crosses the yz-plane at the point $\left(0, \frac{17}{2}, \frac{-13}{2}\right)$, then

**Options:**

- **(A)** $a=-5, n=1$
- **(B)** $a=5, b=1$
- **(C)** $a=-5, b=-1$
- **(D)** $a=5, b=-1$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $a=5, b=1$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $\mathrm{y}-\mathrm{z}$ plane divides the join in the ratio $\lambda: 1$
$\begin{aligned} & \Rightarrow\left(\frac{3 \lambda+a}{\lambda+1}, \frac{4 \lambda+1}{\lambda+1}, \frac{b \lambda+6}{\lambda+1}\right) \equiv\left(0 ; \frac{17}{2}, \frac{-13}{2}\right) \\ & \Rightarrow 3 \lambda+a=0, \frac{4 \lambda+1}{\lambda+1}=\frac{17}{2}, \frac{b \lambda+6}{\lambda+1}=\frac{-13}{2} \\ & \Rightarrow \lambda=\frac{-5}{3}, a=5, b=1\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q130. MHT CET 2022 (06 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The distance between parallel lines $\frac{x-1}{2}=\frac{y-2}{-2}=\frac{z-3}{1}$ and $\frac{x}{2}=\frac{y}{-2}=\frac{z}{1}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{2 \sqrt{5}}{3}$ units
- **(B)** $\frac{5 \sqrt{5}}{3}$ units
- **(C)** $\frac{\sqrt{5}}{3}$ units
- **(D)** $\frac{4 \sqrt{5}}{3}$ units

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\frac{5 \sqrt{5}}{3}$ units**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/mht_cet/bjeJpMsIbgNNik2RkdB9reu5fqPVWGZYa1pFiOBJCek.original.fullsize.png">
$\begin{aligned} & \Rightarrow \text { S.D. }=\frac{|8 \hat{i}+5 \hat{j}-6 \hat{k}|}{\sqrt{9}} \\ & \Rightarrow \text { S.D. }=\frac{\sqrt{8^2+5^2+(-6)^2}}{3} \\ & \Rightarrow \text { S.D. }=\frac{\sqrt{125}}{3}=\frac{5 \sqrt{5}}{3} \\ & \end{aligned}$

### Q131. MHT CET 2022 (06 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The line joining points $(3,5,-7)$ and $(-2,1,8)$ meets yz-plane at point

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\left(0, \frac{13}{5}, 2\right)$
- **(B)** $(0,13,2)$
- **(C)** $\left(0, \frac{13}{5},-3\right)$
- **(D)** $\left(0, \frac{-13}{5}, 2\right)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\left(0, \frac{13}{5}, 2\right)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $\mathrm{y}_{\mathrm{z}}$ plane divides the join in the ratio $\lambda: 1$ then
$\frac{\lambda \times(-2)+1 \times 3}{\lambda+1}=0 \Rightarrow \lambda=\frac{3}{2}$
So $\mathrm{y}_{\mathrm{z}}$ plane divides the join in the ratio $3: 2$.
Hence, the required point is
$\begin{aligned} & \left(\frac{3 \times(-2)+2 \times 3}{3+2}, \frac{3 \times 1+2 \times 5}{3+2}, \frac{3 \times 8+2 \times(-7)}{3+2}\right) \\ & =\left(0, \frac{13}{5}, 2\right)\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q132. MHT CET 2022 (06 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The Cartesian equation of the line passing through the point $(-3,0,1)$ and perpendicular to vectors $\hat{i}-2 \widehat{j}+\widehat{k}$ and $2 \hat{i}+\widehat{j}-\widehat{k}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{x+3}{1}=\frac{y}{3}=\frac{z-1}{-5}$
- **(B)** $\frac{x+3}{-1}=\frac{y}{3}=\frac{z-1}{5}$
- **(C)** $\frac{x+3}{1}=\frac{y}{3}=\frac{z-1}{5}$
- **(D)** $\frac{x+3}{1}=\frac{y}{-3}=\frac{z-1}{5}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\frac{x+3}{1}=\frac{y}{3}=\frac{z-1}{5}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \frac{\mathrm{x}+3}{(-2) \times(-1)-1 \times 1}=\frac{\mathrm{y}-0}{2 \times 1-1 \times(-1)}=\frac{\mathrm{z}-1}{1 \times 1-2 \times(-2)} \\ & \Rightarrow \frac{\mathrm{x}+3}{1}=\frac{\mathrm{y}}{3}=\frac{\mathrm{z}-1}{5}\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q133. MHT CET 2022 (06 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A vector $\bar{a}$ has components $2 p$ and 1 with respect to a rectangular Cartesian system. This system is rotated though a certain angle about the origin in the counter clockwise sense. If, with respect to the new system, à has components $p+1$ and 1 , then

**Options:**

- **(A)** $p=0$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{p}=-1$ or $\mathrm{p}=\frac{1}{3}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{p}=1$ or $\mathrm{p}=-\frac{1}{3}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{p}=1$ or $\mathrm{p}=-1$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\mathrm{p}=1$ or $\mathrm{p}=-\frac{1}{3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Magnitude of $\vec{a}$ before rotation = Magnitude of $\vec{a}$ after rotation
$\begin{aligned} & \Rightarrow(2 p)^2+1^2=(p+1)^2+1^2 \\ & \Rightarrow 4 p^2+1=p^2+2 p+1+1 \\ & \Rightarrow 3 p^2-2 p-1=0 \\ & \Rightarrow p=1 \text { or } p=\frac{-1}{3}\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q134. MHT CET 2022 (05 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The acute angle between the lines $x=-y, z=0$ and $x=0, z=0$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{\pi}{3}$
- **(B)** $\frac{\pi}{6}$
- **(C)** $\frac{\pi}{4}$
- **(D)** $\frac{\pi}{18}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\frac{\pi}{4}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given lines are
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \frac{x-0}{1}=\frac{y-0}{-1}=\frac{z-0}{0} \text { and } \frac{x-0}{0}=\frac{y-0}{1}=\frac{z-0}{0} \\
& \text { Now, the angle }=\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{a_1 a_2+b_1 b_2+c_1 c_2}{\sqrt{a_1^2+b_1^2+c_1^2} \cdot \sqrt{a_2^2+b_2^2+c_2^2}}\right) \\
& =\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{1 \times 0+(-1) \times 1+0 \times 0}{\sqrt{1^2+(-1)^2+0^2} \cdot \sqrt{0^2+1^2+0^2}}\right) \\
& =\cos ^{-1}\left|\frac{-1}{\sqrt{2}}\right|[\text { for acute angle }] \\
& =\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\right)=\frac{\pi}{4}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

---

### Q135. MHT CET 2022 (05 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The vector equation of the line passing through the point having position vector $2 \hat{i}+\hat{j}-3 \hat{k}$ and perpendicular to vectors $\hat{i}+\hat{j}+\widehat{k}$ and $\hat{i}+2 \hat{j}-\widehat{k}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\bar{r}=(2 \hat{i}+\hat{j}-3 \hat{k})+\lambda(-3 \hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+\hat{k})$
- **(B)** $\bar{r}=(2 \hat{i}+\hat{j}-3 \hat{k})+\lambda(\hat{i}+2 \hat{j}-\hat{k})$
- **(C)** $\bar{r}=(2 \hat{i}+\hat{j}-3 \hat{k})+\lambda(-3 \hat{i}-2 \hat{j}+\hat{k})$
- **(D)** $\bar{r}=(2 \hat{i}+\hat{j}-3 \widehat{k})+\lambda(-3 \hat{i}+2 \hat{j}-\widehat{k})$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\bar{r}=(2 \hat{i}+\hat{j}-3 \hat{k})+\lambda(-3 \hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+\hat{k})$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The required equation is
$\begin{aligned} & \vec{r}=(2 \hat{i}+\hat{j}-3 \hat{k})+\lambda(\hat{i}+\hat{j}+\hat{k}) \times(\hat{i}+2 \hat{j}-\hat{k}) \\ & \vec{r}=(2 \hat{i}+\hat{j}-3 \hat{k})+\lambda(-3 \hat{i}+2 j+\hat{k})\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q136. MHT CET 2022 (05 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The equation of the line passing through the point $(-1,3,-2)$ and perpendicular to each of the lines $\frac{x}{1}=\frac{y}{2}=\frac{z}{3}$ and $\frac{x+2}{-3}=\frac{y-1}{2}=\frac{z+1}{5}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{x+1}{2}=\frac{y-3}{7}=\frac{z+2}{4}$
- **(B)** $\frac{x+1}{2}=\frac{y+3}{-7}=\frac{z+2}{4}$
- **(C)** $\frac{x-1}{2}=\frac{y+3}{7}=\frac{z-2}{4}$
- **(D)** $\frac{x-1}{2}=\frac{y+3}{-7}=\frac{z-2}{4}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\frac{x+1}{2}=\frac{y+3}{-7}=\frac{z+2}{4}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The direction ratios of the required line can be obtained by
$\begin{aligned} & \frac{\mathrm{a}}{2 \times 5-2 \times 3}=\frac{\mathrm{b}}{-3 \times 3-1 \times 5}=\frac{\mathrm{c}}{1 \times 2-(-3) \times 2} \\ & \Rightarrow\langle\mathrm{a}, \mathrm{b}, \mathrm{c}\rangle \equiv\langle 4,-14,8\rangle \equiv\langle 2,-7,4\rangle\end{aligned}$
Hence, the equation of required line
$\begin{aligned} & \frac{x-(-1)}{2}=\frac{y-3}{-7}=\frac{z-(-2)}{4} \\ & \Rightarrow \frac{x+1}{2}=\frac{y-3}{-7}=\frac{z+2}{4}\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q137. MHT CET 2022 (05 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The angle between the lines $\bar{r}=(3 \hat{i}+2 \hat{j}-4 \hat{k})+\lambda(\hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+2 \hat{k})$ and $\bar{r}=(5 \hat{i}-2 \widehat{k})+\mu(3 \hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+6 \widehat{k})$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{20}{21}\right)$
- **(B)** $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{4}{21}\right)$
- **(C)** $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{16}{21}\right)$
- **(D)** $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{19}{21}\right)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{19}{21}\right)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \text { Required angle }=\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{(\hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+2 \widehat{k}) \cdot(3 \hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+6 \hat{k})}{\sqrt{1^2+2^2+2^2} \times \sqrt{3^2+2^2+6^2}}\right) \\ & {\left[\because \theta=\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{\vec{a} \cdot \vec{b}}{|\vec{a}||\vec{b}|}\right)\right]} \\ & =\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{1 \times 3+2 \times 2+2 \times 6}{3 \times 7}\right) \\ & =\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{19}{21}\right)\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q138. MHT CET 2021 (24 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The co-ordinates of the points on the line $\frac{x+2}{1}=\frac{y-1}{2}=\frac{z+1}{-2}$ at a distance of 12 units from the point $\mathrm{A}(-2,1,-1)$ are

**Options:**

- **(A)** $(2,9,-9),(-6,-7,7)$
- **(B)** $(2,9,7),(6,5,-9)$
- **(C)** $(6,9,-5),(-10,9,-5)$
- **(D)** $(6,-7,3),(-10,9,3)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $(2,9,-9),(-6,-7,7)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\frac{\mathrm{x}+2}{1}=\frac{\mathrm{y}-1}{2}=\frac{\mathrm{z}+1}{-2}=\lambda \quad \ldots$ (say)
Hence coordinates of any point on the given line are $(\lambda-2,2 \lambda+1,-2 \lambda-1)$.
This point is at a distance of 12 units from $(-2,1,-1)$.
$\begin{array}{ll}
\therefore \quad & 12=\sqrt{(\lambda-2+2)^2+(2 \lambda+1-1)^2+(-2 \lambda-1+1)^2} \\
& =\sqrt{\lambda^2+4 \lambda^2+4 \lambda^2}=3 \lambda \\
\therefore & \lambda= \pm 4 \Rightarrow \text { Required point }=(2,9,-9) \text { or }(-6,-7,7)
\end{array}$

</details>

---

### Q139. MHT CET 2021 (24 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The co-ordinates of the perpendicular drawn from the point $2 \hat{i}-\hat{j}+5 \hat{k}$ to the line $\vec{r}=(11 \hat{i}-2 \hat{j}-8 \hat{k})+\lambda(10 \hat{i}-4 \hat{j}-11 \hat{k})$ are

**Options:**

- **(A)** $(1,-2,3)$
- **(B)** $(1,2,-3)$
- **(C)** $(-1,2,3)$
- **(D)** $(1,2,3)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $(1,2,3)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

We have $\bar{r}=(11 \hat{i}-2 \hat{j}-8 \hat{k})+\lambda(10 \hat{i}-4 \hat{j}-11 \hat{k})$
So coordinates of any point on this line are $[(10 \lambda+11),(-11 \lambda-2)$,
$$
(-11 \lambda-8)]
$$
Let $\mathrm{P} \equiv(2,-1,5)$ and let $\underline{\mathrm{M}}$ be foot of perpendicular.
$\therefore$ d.r. of PM are $(10 \lambda+9),(-4 \lambda-1),(-11 \lambda-13)$
Sibce $\mathrm{PM}$ is perpendicular to given line, we write
$$
\begin{aligned}
& (10 \lambda+9)(10)+(4 \lambda-1)(-4)+(-11 \lambda-13)(-11)=0 \\
& \therefore 100 \lambda+90+16 \lambda+4+121 \lambda+143=0 \Rightarrow 237=-237 \lambda \\
& \Rightarrow \lambda=-1 \\
& M=-10+11,4-2,11-8) \text { i.e. }(1,2,3)
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

---

### Q140. MHT CET 2021 (23 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The distance between the parallel lines $\frac{x-2}{2}=\frac{y-4}{5}=\frac{z-1}{2}$ and $\frac{x-1}{3}=\frac{y+1}{5}=\frac{z+3}{2}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{1}{\sqrt{38}}$ units
- **(B)** $\sqrt{\frac{333}{38}}$ units
- **(C)** $\sqrt{\frac{300}{37}}$ units
- **(D)** $\sqrt{\frac{300}{35}}$ units

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\sqrt{\frac{333}{38}}$ units**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $\overline{\mathrm{a}}_1=2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+4 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}$ and $\overline{\mathrm{a}}_2=\hat{\mathrm{i}}-2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-3 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$
$\therefore \overline{\mathrm{a}}_2-\overline{\mathrm{a}}_1=-\hat{\mathrm{i}}-6 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-4 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$ and let $\overline{\mathrm{b}}=3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+5 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \overline{\mathrm{b}}\left|\overline{\mathrm{a}}_2-\overline{\mathrm{a}}_1\right|=\left|\begin{array}{ccc}
\hat{\mathrm{i}} & \hat{\mathrm{j}} & \hat{\mathrm{k}} \\
3 & 5 & 2 \\
-1 & -6 & -4
\end{array}\right|=\hat{\mathrm{i}}(-20+12)-\hat{\mathrm{j}}(-12+2)+\hat{\mathrm{k}}(-18+5) \\
& =-8 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+10 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-13 \hat{\mathrm{k}} \\
& \therefore\left|\overline{\mathrm{b}} \times\left(\overline{\mathrm{a}}_2-\overline{\mathrm{a}}_1\right)\right|=\sqrt{64+100+169}=\sqrt{133}
\end{aligned}
$$
Also $|\overline{\mathrm{b}}|=\sqrt{9+25+4}=\sqrt{38}$
$\therefore$ Distance between given lines $=\sqrt{\frac{333}{38}}$ units

</details>

### Q141. MHT CET 2021 (23 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The vector equation of the line passing through $\mathrm{P}(1,2,3)$ and $\mathrm{Q}(2,3,4)$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $(\hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k})+\lambda(\hat{i}+\hat{j}+\hat{k})$
- **(B)** $(\hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k})+\lambda(\hat{i}-\hat{j}-\hat{k})$
- **(C)** $(\hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k})+\lambda(2 \hat{i}+3 \hat{j}+4 \hat{k})$
- **(D)** $(\hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k})+\lambda(2 \hat{i}+6 \hat{j}+12 \hat{k})$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $(\hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k})+\lambda(\hat{i}+\hat{j}+\hat{k})$**

d.r. of line through PQ are $[(2,-1),(3,-2),(4,-3)]$ i.e. $(1,1,1)$ Hence required equation of line is

(\hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k})+\lambda(\hat{i}+\hat{j}+\hat{k})
$$

### Q142. MHT CET 2021 (23 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The length of perpendicular drawn from the point $2 \hat{i}-\hat{j}+5 \hat{k}$ to the line $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=(11 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-8 \hat{\mathrm{k}})+\lambda(10 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-4 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-11 \hat{\mathrm{k}})$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\sqrt{14}$ units
- **(B)** 14 units
- **(C)** 237 units
- **(D)** $\sqrt{237}$ units

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\sqrt{14}$ units**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $\mathrm{P}=(2,-1,5)$ and co-ordinates of any point on the given line be
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{Q} \equiv(10 \lambda+11,-4 \lambda-2,-11 \lambda-13) \\
& \text { d.r. of } \mathrm{PQ} \text { are }(10 \lambda+9,-4 \lambda-1,-11 \lambda-13) \\
& \text { d.r. of given line are }(10,-4,-11) \\
& \therefore(10 \lambda+9)(10)+(-4 \lambda-1)(-4)+(-11 \lambda-13)(-11)=0 \\
& \therefore 100 \lambda+90+16 \lambda+4+121 \lambda+143=0 \Rightarrow \lambda=-1 \\
& \therefore \mathrm{Q} \equiv(1,2,3) \text { and } \mathrm{d}(\mathrm{PQ})=\sqrt{1^2+3^2+2^2}=\sqrt{14} \text { units }
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q143. MHT CET 2021 (23 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the lines $\frac{x-1}{2}=\frac{y+1}{3}=\frac{z-1}{4}$ and $\frac{x-2}{1}=\frac{y+m}{2}=\frac{z-2}{1}$ intersect each other, then value of $m$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1
- **(B)** -2
- **(C)** 2
- **(D)** -1

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) -1**

**Detailed Solution:**

Two intersection lines are
$$
\frac{x-1}{2}=\frac{y+1}{3}=\frac{z-1}{4}=\lambda \text { and } \frac{x-2}{1}=\frac{y+m}{2}=\frac{z-2}{1}=\mu
$$
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/mht_cet/KEdrD1x8zMDKP9FkPKpTkMtjAdBXgFfNxzxx4a1XG6Y.original.fullsize.png">

From (1) and (2) $\lambda=0$ and $\mu=-1$ Then from (3), we get $m=-1$

</details>

### Q144. MHT CET 2021 (21 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The equations of a line passing through $(3,-1,2)$ and perpendicular to the lines $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=(\hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}-\hat{\mathrm{k}})+\lambda(2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}})$ and $\bar{r}=(2 \hat{i}+\hat{j}-3 \hat{k})+\mu(\hat{i}-2 \hat{j}+2 \hat{k})$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{x-3}{2}=\frac{y+1}{3}=\frac{z-2}{2}$
- **(B)** $\frac{x-3}{3}=\frac{y+1}{2}=\frac{z-2}{2}$
- **(C)** $\frac{x+3}{2}=\frac{y+1}{3}=\frac{z-2}{2}$
- **(D)** $\frac{x-3}{2}=\frac{y+1}{3}=\frac{z-2}{3}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\frac{x-3}{2}=\frac{y+1}{3}=\frac{z-2}{2}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $a, b, c$ be the direction ratios of the required line.
$$
\therefore 2 \mathrm{a}-2 \mathrm{~b}+\mathrm{c}=0
$$
... (1) and $a-2 b+2 c=0$
From (1) and (2), we write
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \frac{\mathrm{a}}{\left|\begin{array}{ll}
-2 & 1 \\
-2 & 1
\end{array}\right|}=\frac{\mathrm{b}}{\left|\begin{array}{ll}
2 & 1 \\
1 & 2
\end{array}\right|}=\frac{\mathrm{c}}{\left|\begin{array}{ll}
2 & -2 \\
1 & -2
\end{array}\right|} \quad \Rightarrow \frac{\mathrm{a}}{-2}=\frac{\mathrm{b}}{-3}=\frac{\mathrm{c}}{-2} \\
& \therefore(\mathrm{a}, \mathrm{b}, \mathrm{c})=(2,3,2)
\end{aligned}
$$
So equation of required line is
$$
\frac{x-3}{2}=\frac{y+1}{3}=\frac{z-2}{2}
$$

</details>

---

### Q145. MHT CET 2021 (21 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The distance between parallel lines $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=(2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-\hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}})+\lambda(2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}-\hat{\mathrm{k}})$ and $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=(\hat{\mathrm{i}}-\hat{\mathrm{j}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{k}})+\mu(2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}-2 \hat{\mathrm{k}})$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\sqrt{2}$
- **(B)** $\frac{1}{3}$
- **(C)** $\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}$ units
- **(D)** $\frac{\sqrt{2}}{3}$ units

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\frac{\sqrt{2}}{3}$ units**

**Detailed Solution:**

The distance between given parallel lines
$$
\begin{aligned}
& =\left|\frac{[(\hat{\mathrm{i}}-2 \hat{\mathrm{i}})+(-\hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{j}})+(2 \hat{\mathrm{k}}-\hat{\mathrm{k}})] \times(2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}-2 \hat{\mathrm{k}})}{|2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}-2 \hat{\mathrm{k}}|}\right|=\left|\frac{(-\hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}) \times(2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}-2 \hat{\mathrm{k}})}{\sqrt{(2)^2+(1)^2+(-2)^2}}\right| \\
& (-\hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}) \times(2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}-2 \hat{\mathrm{k}})=\left|\begin{array}{ccc}
\mathrm{i} & \hat{\mathrm{j}} & \hat{\mathrm{k}} \\
-1 & 0 & 1 \\
2 & 1 & -2
\end{array}\right|=\hat{\mathrm{i}}(0-1)-\hat{\mathrm{j}}(2-2)+\hat{\mathrm{k}}(-1-0)=-\hat{\mathrm{i}}-\hat{\mathrm{k}} \\
& \therefore \mathrm{d}=\left|\frac{\mid-\hat{\mathrm{i}}-\hat{\mathrm{k}}}{\sqrt{9}}\right|=\frac{\sqrt{2}}{3} \text { units }
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q146. MHT CET 2021 (21 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the line $\frac{1-x}{3}=\frac{7 y-14}{2 \lambda}=\frac{z-3}{2}$ and $\frac{7-7 x}{3 \lambda}=\frac{y-5}{1}=\frac{6-z}{5}$ are at right angles, then $\lambda=$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{-70}{11}$
- **(B)** $\frac{70}{11}$
- **(C)** $\frac{11}{70}$
- **(D)** $\frac{-11}{70}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\frac{70}{11}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

We have lines
$$
\frac{x-1}{-3}=\frac{7(y-2)}{2 \lambda}=\frac{z-3}{2} \text { and } \frac{7(1-x)}{3 \lambda}=\frac{y-5}{1}=\frac{z-6}{-5}
$$
i.e. $\frac{x-1}{-3}=\frac{y-2}{\left(\frac{2 \lambda}{7}\right)}=\frac{z-3}{2}$ and $\frac{x-1}{\left(\frac{-3 \lambda}{7}\right)}=\frac{y-5}{1}=\frac{z-6}{5}$
Since given lines are at right angles, we write
$$
\begin{aligned}
& (-3)\left(\frac{-3 \lambda}{7}\right)+\left(\frac{2 \lambda}{7}\right)(1)+(2)(-5)=0 \\
& \therefore \frac{9 \lambda}{7}+\frac{2 \lambda}{7}-10=0 \Rightarrow 11 \lambda=70 \Rightarrow \lambda=\frac{70}{11}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

---

### Q147. MHT CET 2021 (20 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The Cartesian equation of the lien passing through the points $\mathrm{A}$ $(2,2,1)$ and $B(1,3,0)$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{\mathrm{x}+2}{1}=\frac{\mathrm{y}+2}{-1}=\frac{\mathrm{z}+1}{-1}$
- **(B)** $\frac{\mathrm{x}-2}{-1}=\frac{\mathrm{y}-2}{1}=\frac{\mathrm{z}-1}{-1}$
- **(C)** $\frac{x+2}{-1}=\frac{y+2}{1}=\frac{z+1}{-1}$
- **(D)** None of these

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\frac{\mathrm{x}-2}{-1}=\frac{\mathrm{y}-2}{1}=\frac{\mathrm{z}-1}{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The required Cartesian equation of line is $\frac{x-2}{1-2}=\frac{y-2}{3-2}=\frac{z-1}{0-1}$ i.e. $\frac{x-2}{-1}=\frac{y-2}{1}=\frac{z-1}{-1}$

</details>

### Q148. MHT CET 2021 (20 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The parametric equations of a line passing through the points $\mathrm{A}$ $(3,4,-7)$ and $B(1,-1,6)$ are

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{x}=3+\lambda, \mathrm{y}=-1+4 \lambda, \mathrm{z}=-7+6 \lambda$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{x}=-2+3 \lambda, \mathrm{y}=-5+4 \lambda, \mathrm{z}=13-7 \lambda$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{x}=3-2 \lambda, \mathrm{y}=4-5 \lambda, \mathrm{z}=-7+13 \lambda$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{x}=3-2 \lambda, \mathrm{y}=4-5 \lambda, \mathrm{z}=-7+13 \lambda$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\mathrm{x}=3-2 \lambda, \mathrm{y}=4-5 \lambda, \mathrm{z}=-7+13 \lambda$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Equation of required line is
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \frac{x-3}{1-3}=\frac{y-4}{-1-4}=\frac{z+7}{6+7}=\lambda \ldots \text { (Say) } \\
& \therefore \frac{x-3}{-2}=\frac{y-4}{-5}=\frac{z+7}{13}=\lambda \\
& \therefore x=-2 \lambda+3, y=-5 \lambda+4, z=13 \lambda-7
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

---

### Q149. MHT CET 2020 (20 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the line $\frac{x-1}{-3}=\frac{y-2}{2 k}=\frac{z-3}{2}$ and $\frac{x-1}{3 k}=\frac{y-5}{1}=\frac{z-6}{-5}$ are perpendicular to each other, then $k$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{7}{10}$
- **(B)** $\frac{10}{7}$
- **(C)** $\frac{-7}{10}$
- **(D)** $\frac{-10}{7}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\frac{-10}{7}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given lines are perpendicular. Hence we write
$$
\begin{aligned}
&(-3)(3 \mathrm{k})+(2 \mathrm{k})(1)+(2)(-5)=0 \\
\therefore &-9 \mathrm{k}+2 \mathrm{k}-10=0 \\
\therefore & 7 \mathrm{k}=-10 \Rightarrow \quad \mathrm{k}=\frac{-10}{7}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

---

### Q150. MHT CET 2020 (20 Oct Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the lines $\frac{x-1}{2}=\frac{\mathrm{y}+1}{3}=\frac{\mathrm{z}-1}{4}$ and $\frac{x-3}{1}=\frac{\mathrm{y}-\mathrm{k}}{2}=\frac{\mathrm{z}}{1}$ interect, then the value of the $\mathrm{k}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\times$. $\frac{-2}{9}$
- **(B)** $\sqrt{2} \frac{9}{2}$
- **(C)** $\times$ 3. $\frac{3}{2}$
- **(D)** $\times 4 \cdot \frac{-3}{2}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\sqrt{2} \frac{9}{2}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $\left(\mathrm{x}_{1}, \mathrm{y}_{1}, \mathrm{z}_{1}\right) \equiv(1,-1,1)$ and $\left(\mathrm{x}_{2}, \mathrm{y}_{2}, \mathrm{z}_{2}\right) \equiv(3, \mathrm{k}, 0)$
Let $\left(\mathrm{a}_{1}, \mathrm{~b}_{1}, \mathrm{c}_{1}\right) \equiv(2,3,4)$ and let $\left(\mathrm{a}_{2}, \mathrm{~b}_{2}, \mathrm{c}_{2}\right) \equiv(1,2,1)$
Since, the lines intersect
$\left|\begin{array}{ccc}x_{2}-x_{1} & y_{2}-y_{1} & z_{2}-z_{1} \\ a_{1} & b_{1} & c_{1} \\ a_{2} & b_{2} & c_{2}\end{array}\right|=0$
$\left|\begin{array}{ccc}2 & k+1 & -1 \\ 2 & 3 & 4 \\ 1 & 2 & 1\end{array}\right|=0$
$2(3-8)-(k+1)(2-4)-1(4-3)=0$
$-10+2 k+2-1=0 \Rightarrow k=\frac{9}{2}$

</details>

---

### Q151. MHT CET 2020 (20 Oct Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The parametric equation of the line passing throught the points $A(3,4,-7)$ and $B(1,-1,6)$ are

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x=1+3 \lambda, \quad y=-1+4 \lambda, \quad z=6-7 \lambda$
- **(B)** $x=-2+3 \lambda, \mathrm{y}=-5+4 \lambda, \quad \mathrm{z}=13-7 \lambda$
- **(C)** $x=3-2 \lambda, y=4-5 \lambda, \quad \mathrm{z}=-7+13 \lambda$
- **(D)** $x=3+\lambda, \quad \mathrm{y}=-1+4 \lambda, \quad \mathrm{z}=-7+6 \lambda$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $x=3-2 \lambda, y=4-5 \lambda, \quad \mathrm{z}=-7+13 \lambda$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Cartesian Equation is
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \frac{x-x_{1}}{x_{1}-x_{2}}=\frac{y-y_{1}}{y_{1}-y_{2}}=\frac{z-z_{1}}{z_{1}-z_{2}} \text { i.e. } \\
& \frac{x-3}{3-1}=\frac{y-4}{4+1}=\frac{z+7}{-7-6} \Rightarrow \frac{x-3}{2}=\frac{y-4}{5}=\frac{z+7}{-13}=\lambda \quad \text {...say } \\
\therefore & \frac{x-3}{-2}=\frac{y-4}{-5}=\frac{z+7}{13}=\lambda \Rightarrow x=3-2 \lambda, y=4-5 \lambda, z=-7+13 \lambda
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

---

### Q152. MHT CET 2020 (19 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The equation of the line passing through $(1,2,3)$ and perpendicular to the lines
$x-1=\frac{y+2}{2}=\frac{z+4}{4}$ and $\frac{x-1}{2}=\frac{y-2}{2}=z+3$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{x-1}{6}=\frac{2-y}{7}=\frac{z-3}{2}$
- **(B)** $\frac{x-1}{6}=\frac{y-2}{7}=\frac{z-3}{2}$
- **(C)** $\frac{x-1}{4}=\frac{2-y}{5}=\frac{z-3}{2}$
- **(D)** $x-1=\frac{y-2}{2}=\frac{z-3}{4}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\frac{x-1}{6}=\frac{2-y}{7}=\frac{z-3}{2}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

(D)
The vector perpendicular to both the given lines is given by $\left|\begin{array}{lll}\hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1 & 2 & 4 \\ 2 & 2 & 1\end{array}\right|=\hat{i}(-6)-\hat{j}(-7)+\hat{k}(-2)=-6 \hat{i}+7 \hat{j}-2 \hat{k}$
Hence d.r.s. of required line are $6,-7,2$.
Thus eq. of required line is
$\frac{x-1}{6}=\frac{y-2}{-7}=\frac{2-3}{2} \text { i.e. } \frac{x-1}{6}=\frac{2-y}{7}=\frac{z-3}{2}$

</details>

### Q154. MHT CET 2020 (16 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The angle between the lines $\frac{x-1}{4}=\frac{y-3}{1}=\frac{z}{8}$ and $\frac{x-2}{2}=\frac{y+1}{2}=\frac{z-4}{1}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{3}{4}\right)$
- **(B)** $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{3}\right)$
- **(C)** $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)$
- **(D)** $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{2}{3}\right)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{2}{3}\right)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

(D)
Let $\bar{a}$ and $\bar{b}$ be the vectors in the direction of the lines $\frac{x-1}{4}=\frac{y-3}{1}=\frac{z}{8}$ and $\frac{x-2}{2}=\frac{y+1}{2}=\frac{z-4}{1}$ respectively.
$\therefore \quad \overline{\mathrm{a}}=4 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}+8 \hat{\mathrm{k}} \quad$ and $\quad \overline{\mathrm{b}}=2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}$
$\bar{a} \cdot \bar{b}=(4 \times 2)+(1 \times 2)+(8 \times 1)=8+2+8=18$
Also $|\bar{a}| \quad=\sqrt{16+1+64}=\sqrt{81}=9$ and $|\bar{b}|=\sqrt{4+1+4}=\sqrt{9}=3$
Let $\theta$ be the acute angle between the two given lines.
$\cos \theta=\frac{\bar{a} \cdot \bar{b}}{|\bar{a}| \cdot|\bar{b}|}=\frac{18}{9 \times 3}=\frac{2}{3} \Rightarrow \theta=\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{2}{3}\right)$

</details>

---

### Q155. MHT CET 2020 (16 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The cosine of the angle included between the lines $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=(2 \hat{\imath}+\hat{\jmath}-2 \hat{k})+\lambda(\hat{\imath}-2 \hat{\jmath}-2 \hat{k})$ and $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=(\hat{\imath}+\hat{\jmath}+3 \hat{k})+\mu(3 \hat{\imath}+2 \hat{\jmath}-6 \hat{k})$
where $\lambda, \mu \in \mathrm{R}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{13}{21}$
- **(B)** $\frac{11}{21}$
- **(C)** $\frac{3}{21}$
- **(D)** $\frac{17}{21}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\frac{11}{21}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

(D)
$\tan ^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{3}\right)+\tan ^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{5}\right)=\tan ^{-1}\left(\frac{\frac{1}{3}+\frac{1}{5}}{1-\frac{1}{3} \cdot \frac{1}{5}}\right)=\tan ^{-1}\left(\frac{\frac{8}{15}}{\frac{15-1}{15}}\right)=\tan ^{-1}\left(\frac{8}{14}\right)=\tan ^{-1}\left(\frac{4}{7}\right)$ $\tan ^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{7}\right)+\tan ^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{8}\right)=\tan ^{-1}\left(\frac{\frac{1}{7}+\frac{1}{8}}{1-\frac{1}{7} \times \frac{1}{8}}\right)=\tan ^{-1}\left(\frac{\frac{15}{56}}{\frac{55}{56}}\right)=\tan ^{-1}\left(\frac{15}{55}\right)=\tan ^{-1}\left(\frac{3}{11}\right)$ Now,
$\begin{aligned}
\tan ^{-1}\left(\frac{4}{7}\right)+\tan ^{-1}\left(\frac{3}{11}\right) &=\tan ^{-1}\left(\frac{\frac{4}{7}+\frac{3}{11}}{1-\frac{4}{7} \times \frac{3}{11}}\right)=\tan ^{-1}\left(\frac{\frac{44+21}{77}}{\frac{77-12}{77}}\right)=\tan ^{-1}\left(\frac{\frac{65}{77}}{\frac{65}{77}}\right) \\
&=\tan ^{-1}(1)=\frac{\pi}{4}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q156. MHT CET 2020 (16 Oct Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The angle between the lines $\bar{r}=(\hat{\imath}+2 \hat{\jmath}+3 \hat{k})+\lambda(\hat{\imath}+\hat{\jmath}+2 \hat{k})$ and $\bar{r}=(3 \hat{\imath}+\hat{k})+\lambda^{\prime}(2 \hat{\imath}+\hat{\jmath}-\hat{k}), \lambda, \lambda^{\prime} \in \mathrm{R}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{6}\right)$
- **(B)** $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{5}\right)$
- **(C)** $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{3}\right)$
- **(D)** $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{2}{3}\right)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{6}\right)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

(B)
The direction ratios of the lines are $1,1,2$ and $2,1,-1$ and let $\theta$ be the angle between them
$\begin{aligned}
\cos \theta &=\left|\frac{(1)(2)+(1)(1)+2(-1)}{\sqrt{1+1+4} \cdot \sqrt{4+1+1}}\right| \\
\cos \theta &=\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{6} \cdot \sqrt{6}}\right|=\frac{1}{6} \Rightarrow \theta=\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{6}\right)
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q157. MHT CET 2020 (15 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The angle between the two lines
$\frac{x-4}{1}=\frac{y+4}{2}=\frac{z+1}{2}$ and $\frac{x+1}{2}=\frac{y+3}{2}=\frac{z-4}{-1}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{4}{9}\right)$
- **(B)** $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{5}{9}\right)$
- **(C)** $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{9}\right)$
- **(D)** $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{2}{9}\right)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{4}{9}\right)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $\theta$ be the required angle
$$
\begin{array}{l}
\cos \theta=\left|\frac{1(2)+2(2)+2(-1)}{\sqrt{1+4+4} \sqrt{4+4+1}}\right|=\frac{|2+4-2|}{3 \times 3} \\
\cos \theta=\frac{4}{9} \quad \Rightarrow \theta=\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{4}{9}\right)
\end{array}
$$

</details>

### Q158. MHT CET 2020 (15 Oct Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The shortest distance between the lines $1+x=2 y=-12 z$ and
$x=y+2=6 z-6$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1$ unit
- **(B)** $4$ units
- **(C)** $2$ units
- **(D)** $3$ units

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $2$ units**

**Detailed Solution:**

Shortest distance between the lines
$$
\begin{array}{c}
\frac{x+1}{1}=\frac{y}{\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)}=\frac{z}{\left(\frac{-1}{12}\right)} \text { and } \frac{x}{1}=\frac{y+2}{1}=\frac{z-1}{\left(\frac{1}{6}\right)} \text { is } \\
\begin{array}{c}
0+1-2-0 \\
1 \\
\frac{1}{2} & \frac{-1}{12} \\
1 & \frac{1}{6}
\end{array} \mid \\
d=\frac{\sqrt{\left(\frac{1}{6}+\frac{1}{12}\right)^{2}+\left(1-\frac{1}{2}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{1}{12}+\frac{1}{12}\right)^{2}}}{12}
\end{array}
$$
$=\frac{\left|\begin{array}{ccc}1 & -2 & 1 \\ 1 & \frac{1}{2} & \frac{-1}{12} \\ 1 & 1 & \frac{1}{6}\end{array}\right|}{\sqrt{\left(\frac{1}{4}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{1}{6}\right)^{2}}}=\frac{(1)\left(\frac{1}{12}+\frac{1}{12}\right)+2\left(\frac{1}{6}+\frac{1}{12}\right)+\left(1-\frac{1}{2}\right) \mid}{\sqrt{\frac{1}{16}+\frac{1}{4}+\frac{1}{36}}}$
$=\frac{\left|\frac{1}{6}+\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\right|}{\sqrt{\frac{9+36+4}{144}}}=\frac{\left|\frac{7}{6}\right|}{\sqrt{\frac{49}{144}}}=\frac{7}{6} \times \frac{12}{7}=2$

</details>

---

### Q159. MHT CET 2020 (15 Oct Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The angle between the lines $\frac{x-1}{4}=\frac{y-3}{1}=\frac{z}{8}$ and $\frac{x-2}{2}=\frac{y+1}{2}=\frac{z-4}{1}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\sin ^{-1}\left(\frac{2}{3}\right)$
- **(B)** $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{2}{3}\right)$
- **(C)** $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{3}\right)$
- **(D)** $\sin ^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{3}\right)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{2}{3}\right)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\cos \theta=\left|\frac{4(2)+1(2)+8(1)}{\sqrt{4^{2}+1^{2}+8^{2}} \sqrt{2^{2}+2^{2}+1^{2}}}\right|=\left|\frac{8+1+8}{\sqrt{81} \cdot \sqrt{9}}\right|=\frac{18}{27}$
$\therefore \cos \theta=\frac{2}{3} \Rightarrow \theta=\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{2}{3}\right)$

</details>

### Q160. MHT CET 2020 (14 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The equation of line passing through the points $(3,4,-7)$ and $(6,-1,1)$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{x-3}{3}=\frac{y-4}{-5}=\frac{z+7}{8}$
- **(B)** $\frac{x-3}{3}=\frac{y-4}{5}=\frac{z+7}{8}$
- **(C)** $\frac{x-3}{-3}=\frac{y-4}{-5}=\frac{z+7}{8}$
- **(D)** $\frac{x-3}{3}=\frac{y-4}{-5}=\frac{z-7}{8}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\frac{x-3}{3}=\frac{y-4}{-5}=\frac{z+7}{8}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Required eq. of line is
$$
\frac{x-3}{6-3}=\frac{y-4}{-1-4}=\frac{z+7}{1+7} \text { i.e. } \frac{x-3}{3}=\frac{y-4}{-5}=\frac{z+7}{8}
$$

</details>

### Q161. MHT CET 2020 (14 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The co-ordinates of the foot of the perpendicular from the point $(0,2,3)$ on the line
$\frac{x+3}{5}=\frac{y-1}{2}=\frac{z+4}{3}$ are

**Options:**

- **(A)** $(-2,-3,1)$
- **(B)** $(2,3,-1)$
- **(C)** $(2,3,1)$
- **(D)** (-2,-3,-1)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $(2,3,-1)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $\alpha$ be the foot of the Ler drawn from the point $\mathrm{P}(0,2,3)$ to the given line.
Co-ordinates of any point on given line are $\frac{x+3}{5}=\frac{y-1}{2}=\frac{z+4}{3}=\lambda$...say
Let $Q \equiv(5 \lambda-3,2 \lambda+1,3 \lambda-4)$ and $P \equiv(0,2,3)$
$\therefore$ d.r. of PQ are $5 \lambda-3,2 \lambda-1,3 \lambda-7$
d.r. of given line are $5,2,3$
$\therefore 5(5 \lambda-3)+2(2 \lambda-1)+3(3 \lambda-7)=0$
$\Rightarrow 25 \lambda-15+4 \lambda-2+9 \lambda-21=0 \Rightarrow 38 \lambda-38=0 \Rightarrow \lambda=1$
$\therefore \mathrm{Q} \equiv(2,1,-4)$
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/mht_cet/Vr91t5xwTXxj_2fEmuNJ_tX7mEp7b8xBMizE-vAXG0A.original.fullsize.png">

</details>

### Q163. MHT CET 2020 (14 Oct Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the lines given by $\bar{r}=2 \hat{\imath}+\lambda(\hat{\imath}+2 \hat{\jmath}+m \hat{k})$ and $\bar{r}=\hat{\imath}+\mu(2 \hat{\imath}+\hat{\jmath}+6 \hat{k})$ are
perpendicular, then the value of $m$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{3}{2}$
- **(B)** $\frac{-3}{2}$
- **(C)** $\frac{2}{3}$
- **(D)** $\frac{-2}{3}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\frac{-2}{3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given lines are $\perp$ er and their d.r.s. are $(1,2, \mathrm{~m})$ and $(2,1,6)$
$\therefore \quad 1(2)+2(1)+m(6)=0$
$\therefore \quad 6 m=-4 \quad \Rightarrow m=\frac{-2}{3}$

</details>

### Q164. MHT CET 2020 (13 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The equation of a line passing through the point $(2,4,6)$ and parallel to the line
$3 x+4=4 y-1=1-4 z$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{x-2}{4}=\frac{y-4}{3}=\frac{z-6}{3}$
- **(B)** $\frac{x-2}{4}=\frac{y-4}{3}=\frac{z-6}{-3}$
- **(C)** $\frac{x-2}{-4}=\frac{y-4}{3}=\frac{z-6}{-3}$
- **(D)** $\frac{x-2}{-4}=\frac{y-4}{-3}=\frac{z-6}{-3}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\frac{x-2}{4}=\frac{y-4}{3}=\frac{z-6}{-3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given equation of line is
$\frac{3 x+4}{x+\frac{4}{3}}=\frac{y-\frac{1}{4}}{\left(\frac{1}{3}\right)}=\frac{z-\frac{1}{4}}{\left(\frac{1}{4}\right)}=\frac{3\left(x+\frac{4}{3}\right)}{\left(-\frac{1}{4}\right)}=\frac{4\left(y-\frac{1}{4}\right)}{1}=\frac{-4\left(z-\frac{1}{4}\right)}{1}$
$\begin{aligned}
& \frac{x+\frac{4}{3}}{\left(\frac{1}{3}\right)}=\frac{y-\frac{1}{4}}{\left(\frac{1}{4}\right)}=\frac{z-\frac{1}{4}}{\left(-\frac{1}{4}\right)} \\
\therefore & \frac{1}{3}, \frac{1}{4},-\frac{1}{4} \text { are d.r. of a line i.e. } 4,3,-3
\end{aligned}$
Hence eq. of required line is
$\frac{x-2}{4}=\frac{y-4}{3}=\frac{z-6}{-3}$

</details>

---

### Q165. MHT CET 2020 (13 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the lines $\frac{x-1}{5}=\frac{y+1}{3}=\frac{3-z}{\lambda}$ and $\frac{x+1}{4}=\frac{1-3 y}{15}=z+1$ are perpendicular
to each other, then $\lambda=$

**Options:**

- **(A)** 2
- **(B)** 3
- **(C)** 5
- **(D)** 4

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 5**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given lines are $\frac{x-1}{5}=\frac{y+1}{3}=\frac{z-3}{-\lambda}$ and $\frac{x+1}{4}=\frac{y-\frac{1}{3}}{-5}=\frac{z+1}{1}$
d.r. of the given lines are $5,3,-\lambda$ and $4,-5,1$
These lines are $\perp$ er
$\therefore 5(4)+3(-5)+(-\lambda)(1)=0 \Rightarrow 20-15-\lambda=0 \Rightarrow \lambda=5$

</details>

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/mht_cet/S5cR08KR9pyhpi6CnvBDOro1I4ss1gztUXugo9pew5A.original.fullsize.png">
